# SOLIDIFY /app/ copy audit

Read-only audit of the user-facing copy in the instrument page (`app/index.html` plus the `src/*.ts` modules that build its UI), 2026-09-23. No repo file was edited and no state-changing git command was run. Line numbers are against the working tree as read today; the repo has concurrent sessions, so re-check a line before editing it.

## 0. Scope and method

**In scope.** `app/index.html` and every `src/*.ts` module reachable from `src/main.ts` by static import, which is 24 files with user strings: main, ui, tour, lab, heatpanel, heattreat, thermal, porosity, composer, elements, alloy, phasediagram, phasedata, materials, units, quant, experiment, optimizer, challenge, analyze, analyze3d, slicepanel, sim, sim3d. hud, share, program, formbits, nucleation, viewcube, render and render3d were read and have no string over 6 words or with an em dash.

**Out of scope.** The landing page modules (landing.ts, landing-motion.ts, dive.ts, dive3d.ts), `shaders.ts` / `shaders3d.ts` (WGSL; every em dash in them is in a comment), mc-worker.ts, code comments and console output. The raw em-dash counts per file are dominated by comments (main.ts has 101 em dashes in the file and 20 in strings; elements.ts 167 and 105), so the counts below are for strings only.

**Extraction.** The TypeScript compiler API walked every string literal and template literal, merged `'a' + 'b'` chains into one logical string, and inlined the text passed through small helpers like `dim(…)` (shown as «…»). A string became a candidate if it has more than 6 words or contains an em dash: 663 from source plus 5 from the HTML, 668 in all.

**Classification.** Each candidate was traced to where it renders (which panel, which element, tooltip or not, reachable or dead) by reading the consumer code, then given a category and a proposed action. Three slices were annotated in parallel (composer/elements/phase diagram; alloy/materials/units/misc; lab/heat treat/thermal) and the rail, main, tour and HTML slice by hand; all four were merged and checked for coverage (668 of 668 rows annotated, none twice).

Categories: **(a)** label, **(b)** descriptor/help text under a control, **(c)** dynamic status/readout, **(d)** tooltip/title attribute, **(e)** tour/learn content, **(f)** honesty caveat (says what the model cannot do, refuses, or flags a number as unsourced), **(x)** not user-visible. Rows can carry two tags ("f+b"); the primary tag is first.

Actions: **DELETE** (the label says it already), **SHORTEN** (terse label/value/unit form), **MOVE-TO-LEARN** (off the instrument; a 1 to 2 sentence student version given, no em dashes), **KEEP-TERSE** (a caveat that must stay on screen, cut to its residue), **DE-DASH** (content stays, dashes go), **KEEP**. For caveats moved to learn mode, the proposal reads `[instrument: …] learn: …`, where the bracket is what must stay visible.

**Limits.** Strings of 6 words or fewer with no em dash are not inventoried (section 7 lists the wordy ones among them). Categories and proposals are editorial calls, not measurements. For large data tables (elements.ts, alloy.ts, phasedata.ts) the "where" names the renderer; some branches were judged unreachable from the UI by reading call sites, not by driving the app. Proposals that touch test-pinned strings are flagged, but none were run against the suite.

## Headline findings

1. **The em dashes are in five places, not one.** 372 prose em dashes sit in 323 user-visible strings: elements.ts 69 (composer cell explanations), tour.ts 66, alloy.ts 49 (composer and #matcaveat caveats), heatpanel.ts 48, ui.ts 28, then lab.ts 22, materials.ts 22 and phasediagram.ts 15. Another 25 are a lone "—" used as the empty value of a readout. That is a symbol, not prose, and 4 of them are test-pinned.
2. **The rail complaint is the easy part.** 70 user-visible strings render in the rail, 38 of them descriptors, carrying 58 em dashes. Almost all are MOVE-TO-LEARN or SHORTEN, and none of the ui.ts descriptors is test-pinned. The three examples the owner quoted are ui.ts:439 (MODES note), materials.ts:51 (MATERIAL note) and ui.ts:937 (cooling-rate note). The whole class is the materials.ts `note` / `si.source` fields plus about 15 ui.ts `matnote` divs.
3. **Descriptors: 74 strings carry a (b) tag** (50 as primary). Proposed: 34 SHORTEN, 34 MOVE-TO-LEARN, 5 KEEP-TERSE, 1 DELETE.
4. **Honesty caveats are the bulk: 270 user-visible strings are (f).** Proposed: 137 KEEP-TERSE, 46 DE-DASH, 44 SHORTEN, 38 MOVE-TO-LEARN (each with an instrument residue), 5 KEEP. None is proposed for deletion outright. The pattern that preserves them is already in the code: `elements.ts` `admit()` returns a terse `line` and a long `sentence`. Generalising that split (terse field on screen, long field in learn mode) is the cleanest route for alloy.ts, heattreat.ts, units.ts and thermal.ts too.
5. **The suite pins a lot of this copy: about 40 gates.** Most assert phrases in function return values (`derive().refusals`, `canTreat().why`, `admit().line`, `units.regime()`), so moving a sentence to learn mode is safe and rewording it is not. Four gates pin em dashes directly, so a blanket dash replace fails CI (ALLOY-OPEN-IDENTITY, GRID-REASON-LINE) and the local suite (LAB4); UNITS-ABSTRACT pins the null glyph. Several gates set length floors (40 to 190 characters for grid lines, 60 or more for not-grown lines), so those caveats cannot shrink to three words. See section 4.
6. **tour.ts is read as a document by CI.** PD-DOC-CONSTANTS requires "1.65 wt%", "577 °C", "eleven material identities" and "nine one-tap situations" in tour.ts. PD-CHAPTER-PURE requires the chapter "The line you can cross" with "1.65", "577", "(Si)" and "eutectic constituent". The same gate bans a list of retracted claims. De-dashing the tour is safe; rewriting those chapters needs the list open.
7. **The tour is long for a learn channel:** 64 body/watch strings, 3,162 words, median 43 words, 19 over 60 words and 10 over 80. The longest are tour:313 (131 words), tour:400 (128), tour:295 (120) and tour:461 (118). If the learn text moves here, the tour needs trimming at the same time.
8. **Spelling and naming drift.** The prose is mostly British (mould, programme, colour, aluminium, vapour, modelled) but the labels and verbs are partly American (homogenize, analyze, anodize, galvanized, and "aluminum" as the base and material label). "aluminum" is interpolated into sentences that hard-code "aluminium", so both spellings can show in one panel. Three British spellings are test-pinned: "permanent mould · die casting", "not modelled" and "dialled". The four modes go by at least three names each.
9. **ALL-CAPS control names in prose do not match the controls.** The tour and several notes name controls in capitals (COOLING RATE, PULL SPEED, TWIN RATE, POUR AND RUN, ENGINEERING · ML MODE, HEAT TREAT, LAB), but those buttons and sliders are lowercase on screen (the lens buttons and section headers really are uppercase). "ENGINEERING · ML MODE" and "SI" (tour:295) do not match any on-screen text.
10. **Small honesty and polish items found along the way (section 5d):**
    - A milestone code "C3b" and a repo path "docs/PHASE-AUDIT.md" are shown to users.
    - A duplicated section heading.
    - pdsrc shows 240 characters of audit prose cut off mid-sentence.
    - The SEM lens info bar prints fixed, invented instrument metadata ("15 kV · ×240 · WD 10.2 mm").

## 4. Test couplings: what the suite pins in the UI copy

How this was found: every string literal and regex literal in `scripts/*.mjs` was matched against every string in the in-scope source (literal substring, whole regex, and regex fragments ≥ 10 characters so a probe still hits a template with `${…}` in it). Each hit was then read in context to find the gate and what it asserts. False positives (log labels like `"STEREOLOGY"` in verify-3d, enum keys like `deprCap`, element symbols) are dropped. Most gates read **function return values** (`canTreat().why`, `derive().refusals`, `admit().line`, `units.regime()`), not the page, so moving a sentence into learn mode does not by itself break them; **rewording** it does.

"CI" = one of the 13 browser-free scripts GitHub CI runs (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`). "local" = `npm test` only (needs the WebGPU Chrome on this machine).

### 4a. Gates that pin an em dash (a blanket "— → something" replace breaks these)

| gate | script:line | suite | pinned text (source) | a rewrite must |

| ALLOY-OPEN-IDENTITY | verify-composer-grid.mjs:86-118 (check at :178) | CI | the `d.clamps` strings, byte for byte, in a reference table: "Q saturates the model (k floored) — refinement still shows", "strong alloy — model depression capped so growth stays watchable", "liquidus raised (peritectic-dominated) — model runs it as a weak depressant", "composition saturates the model solute field" (alloy.ts:910-923) | update every REF row in the same commit. The gate's docblock says the reference was measured on a pre-P5 tree; a copy change means re-baselining it on purpose, and saying so in the commit |

### 4c. Length floors the suite enforces (how terse a caveat is allowed to get)

| string family | floor / ceiling | gate |
|---|---|---|
| composer grid "why" line | 40 to 190 characters | GRID-REASON-LINE |
| notGrown (phase the solver does not grow) | ≥ 60 characters, must contain "grow" | ALLOY-PHASES-NAMED |
| phase / bound `source` | ≥ 40 characters | PD-REGIME-EXACT, PD-CAP-CEILING |
| derive / decodeMix refusal | > 20 characters | ALLOY-REFUSE-NAMED |
| `canTreat` refusal | > 20 characters, distinct in first 58 | HT-REFUSE |
| element sentence | > 25 characters | EL-TIER-TOTAL |
| TROUTON: clause | ≥ 80 characters | EL-TROUTON-CROSSCHECK |

These floors sit well under the current lengths (most of these strings run 150 to 600 characters), so the terse rewrites proposed in section 7 fit, but "[instrument: 3 words]" is not possible for these families; the instrument residue has to stay a real sentence of 40 to 60 characters.

### 4d. Spelling the suite pins (a US-spelling pass must edit these gates)

- "permanent mould · die casting" (UNITS-REGIME, exact `===`).
- "not modelled" (HT-REFUSE required word).
- "dialled" ("twins held back while cold work is dialled", HT3-SE-PANEL).
- "vapour" is not pinned as a word, but EL-VAPOUR-ADVISORY bans a tier named `/VAPOU?R|BOIL/`.

### 4e. Non-test couplings a rename would break

- Rail section titles are keys twice over: the tour highlights them by exact title (`hl: ["sec:MELT · PROCESS"]`, tour.ts:266-481, dispatched at main.ts:549), and each section's open/closed state is stored under `localStorage["sol.sec." + title]` (ui.ts:255). ui.ts:734 also looks up `this.sections["VOLUME · 3D"]`. Renaming a section silently breaks the tour's highlight for that chapter and resets every returning user's open/closed state for it.
- The composer tier names (ASSESSED, OUTSIDE-THE-MODEL, REFUSED-PAIR, NOT-A-SOLUTE) are also CSS selectors in app/index.html:177-202 (`data-tier`).
- Doc pages quote UI copy. verify-alloy PD-DOC-CALIBRATION requires "EXTRAPOLATED GAUGE" in science/index.html; the science page and README quote several composer/grid numbers checked by GRID-DOC-CLAIMS and EL-DOC-CLAIMS. Rewording UI copy does not break those gates, but the docs would then quote text the app no longer shows.

The prose is predominantly British and the control names and technical verbs are partly American. Pick one before the rewrite: every rewritten string is a chance to converge, and the three pinned spellings must change together with their gates.

### 5c. ALL CAPS inside prose

**Control and mode names.** The lens buttons (MELT, ORIENT, ETCH …), section headers (PRESETS, MATERIAL, MELT · PROCESS …) and switch labels (TRUE 3D, VOXEL MODE, 2.5D RELIEF) really are uppercase on screen, so prose that names them in capitals matches. The sliders, buttons and checkboxes are lowercase on screen, so these prose references do **not** match what the reader sees:

- ui.ts:439 "LAB … HEAT TREAT" (buttons read "⚗ lab mode", "♨ heat treat"); ui.ts:488 "use HEAT TREAT"; ui.ts:620 "flip the TRUE 3D switch" (this one matches); ui.ts:721 "the SLICE plane".
- tour.ts, nearly every chapter: SEAWEED, TWIN RATE, INOCULANT, SITES, COOLING RATE, PULL SPEED, COMPOSE ALLOY, **SI** (tour:295, "Drag SI below 1.65"; the composer row reads "Si"), **ENGINEERING · ML MODE** (no control says this), RUN, PAUSE, CHALLENGE, LAB MODE, POUR AND RUN, HEAT TREAT, RESET, REC, SEED, TWIN SEED, CHILL WALL, QUENCH, REHEAT, FREE, BRIDGMAN, WELD, ANISOTROPY δ, FACETED GROWTH, TIP NOISE, LATENT HEAT K, COOLING PROBE, SCHEIL, TEXTURE ROSE, SDAS RULER, PIXEL MODE, 8-BIT, GRAIN STAIN, EBSD FLAT MAP, RESET VIEW, CT SWEEP, STEREOLOGY, POROSITY %, STL, SELECTOR.
- analyze.ts:363 and analyze3d.ts:297 "enable ALLOY"; materials.ts:87 "BRIDGMAN scenario"; optimizer.ts:224 "▶ RUN"; heatpanel.ts:980 "see STEREOLOGY"; lab.ts:742 "in the VOLUME · 3D panels".

A convention that would fix this: name a control in prose exactly as it is printed, and style it (small caps or a code-like chip) instead of upper-casing it.

**Capitals for emphasis (not names):**
- units.ts: DERIVED, NOT, LARGE.
- materials.ts: THE.
- alloy.ts: FIRST, RICHER, **ENTIRELY** (pinned by ALLOY-PHASES-NAMED), EQUILIBRIUM, "NO back-diffusion", THIS, ON.
- elements.ts: **REACTS** (pinned), IS ("**IS this melt**" pinned), NOT, **IDEAL** ("IDEAL solution" pinned), THIS, ATOMS, TRANSITION-METAL, BOTH, DILUTE, DELIBERATE, WITH, OUTSIDE, ONE.
- phasediagram.ts: "DASHED:", TOTAL, NOT.
- phasedata.ts: "PARTLY CALPHAD-SOURCED" (shown in pdsrc).
- heatpanel.ts: GRAIN-GROWTH, SPREAD.
- tour.ts: "THIS instrument".

### 5d. Other copy problems found along the way

- **Internal jargon shown to users:**
  - the milestone code "C3b" (heatpanel.ts:893);
  - a repo path "docs/PHASE-AUDIT.md" (alloy.ts:497);
  - "census", "fabric", "priced", "sourced law" and "Potts model" (heatpanel.ts:1001).
- **Duplicate heading:** "SECTION TABLE — thinnest first" prints twice (lab.ts:638 and :727).
- **pdsrc** (the phase-diagram source line) prints the first 240 characters of the audit `source` field and cuts it mid-sentence. For example, Fe–Ni opens "PARTLY CALPHAD-SOURCED: no cited value…". A separate short display citation would fix it (pattern P5, section 6).
- **ASCII stand-ins** next to typeset forms:
  - "beta-Al3Mg2", "gamma-austenite", "delta-Ni3Nb" and "->" in reactions, while other prose uses β and →;
  - "548.2 C" and "+/-" in pdsrc, while other prose uses °C and ±;
  - hyphen vs en dash in system names ("Cu-O", "Cu-Cu2O" vs "Cu–Pb");
  - ranges as "0.02-0.05" vs "416–671".
- **Units and markup:**
  - the composer solute row reads "wt · at%", with the % missing after wt (composer.ts:495, 504);
  - "ΔT<sub>L</sub>" sits next to "ΔT₀" (composer.ts:557 vs 541).
- **Grammar:** tour:246 "It took the field years to believe him and won the 2011 Nobel Prize". The subject of "won" is the field.
- **SEM info bar (app/index.html:349)** is a fixed string, "SEM · 15 kV · ×240 · WD 10.2 mm · SE". None of those numbers exists in the model: the magnification does not follow zoom, and there is no beam voltage or working distance.
  - It is the one hard-coded instrument claim in an app that otherwise refuses invented numbers.
  - Consider "SEM-style render" plus the real scale bar.
  - It is 6 words, so it is not in the inventory.
- **Dead copy that still costs maintenance.** 142 candidate strings never render. Most are elements.ts `source` notes, alloy.ts `Solute.source` paragraphs, phasedata audit prose, experiment.ts text and the heattreat `SCHEDULES` notes. Several are still test-pinned (HT-REFUSE pins heattreat.ts:738 and :769; EL-TROUTON-CROSSCHECK pins the TROUTON clauses), so they are not free to delete. They are free to leave alone in a copy pass.

## 6. Proposal conventions, and a structural suggestion for learn mode

### 6a. How the proposals in section 8 were written

- **Readout:** an em dash between a value and its gloss becomes " · " (`${rate} · ${regime}`).
- **Consequence:** "X — so Y" becomes "X: Y" or two sentences.
- **Aside:** "X — aside — Y" becomes "X (aside) Y".
- **Refusal:** "subject: reason", e.g. "Cu dropped: weight is not a number", "ΔT₀ refused: k_eff 1.3 outside (0,1) · using aluminum defaults". Offending values and keys stay in, because ALLOY-REFUSE-NAMED, CALIB-MIX-REFUSE and CALIB-POUR-WIRED require them.
- **Caveat kept on screen:** `[instrument: residue] learn: 1-2 sentences`. The residue is the part that must never disappear, such as "not a stress model", "model-only", "no SI identity" or "(Si) not grown".
- **Null glyph:** the lone "—" for a missing value is kept. It is a symbol, and units.ts pins it. If the owner wants zero em dashes anywhere, "n/a" or an en dash would work outside units.ts, but not inside it.
- **Spelling:** spelling in the proposals is not normalised (the slices differ: some say mold/program, some mould/programme). Apply whichever convention 5a settles on.
- **Tour:** DE-DASH rewrites give only the changed sentence, with "…" for the untouched context. Every chapter over about 80 words is flagged TRIM in the notes.

**Composer-specific patterns (from the elements/phase-diagram slice):**
- **P1, the one-liner** (`admit().line`, the grid cell title and gline). It stays on the instrument, with its dashes replaced. It must stay 40 to 190 characters, must not be a prefix of its paragraph, and must keep the BRANCH phrases (section 4).
- **P2, the paragraph** (`admit().sentence`, the DEMIX text). It moves to learn mode without dashes, keeping the EL-TIER-TOTAL rules and branch markers.
- **P3, the vapour advisory.** The instrument form is `⚠ FUME|BOILS · p(X) N atm at T °C, W wt% · pure N atm · ideal-solution estimate`. The no-data case is `p(X) not computed: <reason>`, which keeps the pinned reason phrase and prints no " atm".
- **P4, the size note.** "Hume-Rothery Δr N % (inside/outside ±15 %)" or "Hägg r/R N vs 0.59: inside/outside". The scope clauses go to learn mode.
- **P5, pdsrc.** Add a separate short display citation, e.g. "Murray 1985 · recomputed: pycalphad + MatCalc", and leave `source` as the audit record. verify-phasedata pins its content (PD-SLOPE-CONSISTENT wants "INCONSISTENT" plus "not repaired / NOT RESOLVED"; PD-PRODUCT-SOURCED wants "bracket / DELIBERATELY NULL"), so it cannot be the display string and the audit record at once.
- **P6, pdnotes.** Today several caveats are joined into one run-on line with "  ·  ". Show them as separate terse lines, keeping "off this diagram" and "in the crucible is".

### 6b. The structural suggestion

The copy already has one good model for the instrument/learn split. `elements.ts` `admit()` returns both a terse `line` (≤ 190 characters, gated) and a long `sentence`, and the composer shows the line prominently and the sentence underneath. Generalising that shape is the least risky route to "terse instrument, optional learn mode":

1. For every string producer that feeds a caveat or note, return `{ line, learn }` instead of one sentence. This covers `derive()` refusals, clamps, notGrown and dT0Source in alloy.ts; `canTreat().why` in heattreat.ts; `units.scale.groups[].note` and `regime()` in units.ts; thermal.ts notes; `materials[].note` / `si.source`; and the ui.ts `matnote` divs. The instrument renders `line`, and learn mode also renders `learn`.
2. Point each gate at the field that carries its phrase. Most pinned phrases are facts the terse `line` should keep anyway (the offending key, the ceiling wt%, "not a stress prediction", "no hydrogen-solubility data"). Length floors (section 4c) then apply to `line`, and prose-only pins (ALLOY-PHASES-NAMED's "No fraction is put on it", the HT3-SE-PANEL sentences) move to `learn`.
3. The rail's pure descriptors (the ui.ts `matnote` divs, `materials.note`) have no gate and no consumer except the DOM. They can move to a single learn map keyed by control, rendered by the learn toggle and reused by the tour.

This also removes the main reason the gates pin long sentences today: the long sentence is the only field there is.
